--- SUPERVISOR RESEARCH TAKEOVER ROUND 3 ---

Completed the bounded continuation for selected chapter 1B. No score assigned.

Recovered or confirmed:

- `WEB-1B-013`–`WEB-1B-021`: supervisor-provided safeguards, emergency-response, Angra, INB, PROSUB, and firearms records. These are not currently present in the manifest and should be appended.
- `WEB-1B-022`: IAEA’s 2023 safeguards statement found no indication of diversion of declared nuclear material from peaceful activities in Brazil. [IAEA safeguards statement](https://www.iaea.org/sites/default/files/24/06/20240607_sir_2024_part_ab.pdf)
- `WEB-1B-023`: Brazil’s August 2023 NPT statement is recoverable beyond the prior snippet; it discusses special safeguards procedures for Brazil’s naval nuclear-propulsion program while protecting sensitive information. [MRE NPT statement](https://www.gov.br/mre/pt-br/delbrasaiea/noticias/intervencao-do-brasil-em-ingles-no-debate-sob-o-pilar-ii-nao-proliferacao-na-i-sessao-do-comite-preparatorio-da-xi-conferencia-de-exame-do-tnp-viena-31-7-a-12-8)
- `WEB-1B-024`: Brazil completed recadastration of 939,154 firearms, reported as 99% of the targeted stock. [Ministry of Justice](https://www.gov.br/mj/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/mjsp-atinge-99-de-recadastramento-de-armas-de-fogo)
- `WEB-1B-025`: New firearm registrations fell 79% in 2023, while civilian carry permits fell 56% from 2022; this establishes implementation effect, not crime reduction. [UOL summary of Justice Ministry data](https://noticias.uol.com.br/politica/ultimas-noticias/2024/01/31/lula-dino-prestacao-de-contas.htm)
- `WEB-1B-026`: Federal authorities reported 10,935 illegal-firearm seizures in 2023, up 28% from 2022, but attributed the increase partly to intensified enforcement; this is not evidence that restrictions caused lower violence. [Agência Gov](https://agenciagov.ebc.com.br/noticias/202406/numero-de-apreensoes-de-arma-de-fogo-em-2023-cresce-28-em-relacao-a-2022)

Suggested lens mappings:

- `022`: `1B.3`, `1B.4`, `1B.6`, `1B.10`
- `023`: `1B.2`, `1B.4`, `1B.8`
- `024`: `1B.1`, `1B.5`, `1B.10`
- `025`: `1B.1`, `1B.5`, `1B.10`
- `026`: `1B.1`, `1B.5`, `1B.10`

The evidence now supports a balanced handoff: safeguards had substantial external and operational verification, nuclear emergency systems were exercised, and firearm restrictions were administratively implemented. Remaining limitations are independent evaluation of nuclear oversight, end-to-end transparency in the Angra incident, and causal evidence that firearms restrictions reduced violence or catastrophic risk.
